   written

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>q</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to
   (p,q)}f(x,y)=L}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to (p,q)}f(x,y)=L}

   if the following condition holds:

          For every ε > 0, there exists a δ > 0 such that for all x in S
          and y in T, whenever
          [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
          displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0"> <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <msqrt> <mo
          stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <msup>
          <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo
          stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>q</mi> <msup>
          <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </msqrt> </mrow> <mo><</mo>
          <mi>δ</mi> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
          encoding="application/x-tex">{\textstyle 0<{\sqrt
          {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}<\delta ,}</annotation> </semantics>
          :MATH]
          {\textstyle 0<{\sqrt {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}<\delta ,} we have
          |f(x, y) − L| < ε,^[345][13]

   or formally:
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>δ</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn>
   <mo><</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <msqrt> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <msup> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn>
   </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>−</mo> <mi>q</mi> <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </msqrt> </mrow>
   <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi>L</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo>
   <mi>ε</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon
   >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,(0<{\sqrt
   {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}<\delta \implies |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon
   )).}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall
   x\in S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,(0<{\sqrt {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}<\delta
   \implies |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon )).}

   Here
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <msqrt> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi>
   <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>y</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>q</mi> <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </msqrt>
   </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\textstyle {\sqrt
   {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\textstyle {\sqrt {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}} is the [346]Euclidean
   distance between (x, y) and (p, q). (This can in fact be replaced by
   any [347]norm ||(x, y) − (p, q)||, and be extended to any number of
   variables.)

   For example, we may say
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>,</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mrow> <msup>
   <mi>x</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup>
   <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mi>y</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn>
   </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to
   (0,0)}{\frac {x^{4}}{x^{2}+y^{2}}}=0}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to (0,0)}{\frac {x^{4}}{x^{2}+y^{2}}}=0}
   because for every ε > 0, we can take
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>δ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <msqrt> <mi>ε</mi> </msqrt> </mrow> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\textstyle \delta
   ={\sqrt {\varepsilon }}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\textstyle \delta ={\sqrt {\varepsilon }}} such that for all real x ≠
   0 and real y ≠ 0, if
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0"> <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <msqrt> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>−</mo> <mn>0</mn> <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mn>0</mn> <msup> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn>
   </mrow> </msup> </msqrt> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mo>,</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\textstyle
   0<{\sqrt {(x-0)^{2}+(y-0)^{2}}}<\delta ,}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\textstyle 0<{\sqrt {(x-0)^{2}+(y-0)^{2}}}<\delta ,} then |f(x, y) −
   0| < ε.

   Similar to the case in single variable, the value of f at (p, q) does
   not matter in this definition of limit.

   For such a multivariable limit to exist, this definition requires the
   value of f approaches L along every possible path approaching (p,
   q).^[348][14] In the above example, the function
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mfrac> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>4</mn>
   </mrow> </msup> <mrow> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mi>y</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac>
   </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)={\frac
   {x^{4}}{x^{2}+y^{2}}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)={\frac {x^{4}}{x^{2}+y^{2}}}} satisfies this
   condition. This can be seen by considering the [349]polar coordinates
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo>
   <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>r</mi> <mi>cos</mi> <mo>⁡</mo>
   <mi>θ</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>r</mi> <mi>sin</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mi>θ</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>,</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (x,y)=(r\cos \theta ,r\sin
   \theta )\to (0,0),}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (x,y)=(r\cos \theta ,r\sin \theta )\to (0,0),} which
   gives
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>r</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>r</mi> <mi>cos</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mi>θ</mi>
   <mo>,</mo> <mi>r</mi> <mi>sin</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mi>θ</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>r</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>r</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </msup> <msup> <mi>cos</mi>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>⁡</mo>
   <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> <msup> <mi>r</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <munder> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <msup> <mi>r</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <msup> <mi>cos</mi>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>4</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>⁡</mo>
   <mi>θ</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{r\to 0}f(r\cos
   \theta ,r\sin \theta )=\lim _{r\to 0}{\frac {r^{4}\cos ^{4}\theta
   }{r^{2}}}=\lim _{r\to 0}r^{2}\cos ^{4}\theta .}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{r\to 0}f(r\cos \theta ,r\sin \theta )=\lim _{r\to
   0}{\frac {r^{4}\cos ^{4}\theta }{r^{2}}}=\lim _{r\to 0}r^{2}\cos
   ^{4}\theta .} Here θ = θ(r) is a function of r which controls the shape
   of the path along which f is approaching (p, q). Since cos θ is bounded
   between [−1, 1], by the [350]sandwich theorem, this limit tends to 0.

   In contrast, the function
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mi>y</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>x</mi>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo>
   <msup> <mi>y</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow>
   </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)={\frac
   {xy}{x^{2}+y^{2}}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)={\frac {xy}{x^{2}+y^{2}}}} does not have a limit
   at (0, 0). Taking the path (x, y) = (t, 0) → (0, 0), we obtain
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>t</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mn>0</mn> <msup> <mi>t</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mfrac> </mrow>
   <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{t\to 0}f(t,0)=\lim
   _{t\to 0}{\frac {0}{t^{2}}}=0,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{t\to 0}f(t,0)=\lim _{t\to 0}{\frac
   {0}{t^{2}}}=0,} while taking the path (x, y) = (t, t) → (0, 0), we
   obtain
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>t</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <msup> <mi>t</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mrow> <msup>
   <mi>t</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup>
   <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mi>t</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn>
   </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow>
   <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{t\to 0}f(t,t)=\lim
   _{t\to 0}{\frac {t^{2}}{t^{2}+t^{2}}}={\frac {1}{2}}.}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{t\to 0}f(t,t)=\lim _{t\to 0}{\frac
   {t^{2}}{t^{2}+t^{2}}}={\frac {1}{2}}.}

   Since the two values do not agree, f does not tend to a single value as
   (x, y) approaches (0, 0).

Multiple limits

   [[351]edit]

   Although less commonly used, there is another type of limit for a
   multivariable function, known as the multiple limit. For a two-variable
   function, this is the double limit.^[352][15] Let
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>S</mi>
   <mo>×</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R}
   }</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R} } be defined on
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>S</mi> <mo>×</mo> <mi>T</mi>
   <mo>⊆</mo> <msup> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>,</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle S\times T\subseteq \mathbb
   {R} ^{2},}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle S\times T\subseteq \mathbb {R} ^{2},} we say the double
   limit of f as x approaches p and y approaches q is L, written

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac
   linethickness="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>q</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to p}
   \atop {y\to q}}f(x,y)=L}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to p} \atop {y\to q}}f(x,y)=L}

   if the following condition holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a δ > 0 such that for all x in S and y in
   T, whenever 0 < |x − p| < δ and 0 < |y − q| < δ, we have |f(x, y) − L|
   < ε.^[353][15]

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>δ</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>∧</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>y</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>q</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo>
   <mi>δ</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thickmathspace"
   /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi>L</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo>
   <mi>ε</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall
   \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(\forall y\in
   T)\,((0<|x-p|<\delta )\land (0<|y-q|<\delta )\implies
   |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon ).}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall
   x\in S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,((0<|x-p|<\delta )\land (0<|y-q|<\delta
   )\implies |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon ).}

   For such a double limit to exist, this definition requires the value of
   f approaches L along every possible path approaching (p, q), excluding
   the two lines x = p and y = q. As a result, the multiple limit is a
   weaker notion than the ordinary limit: if the ordinary limit exists and
   equals L, then the multiple limit exists and also equals L. The
   converse is not true: the existence of the multiple limits does not
   imply the existence of the ordinary limit. Consider the example
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mrow> <mo>{</mo> <mtable columnalign="left left" rowspacing=".2em"
   columnspacing="1em" displaystyle="false"> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>1</mn>
   <mspace width="1em" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mtext>for</mtext> </mrow> <mspace width="1em" /> <mi>x</mi> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>≠</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> <mspace
   width="1em" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>for</mtext>
   </mrow> <mspace width="1em" /> <mi>x</mi> <mi>y</mi> <mo>=</mo>
   <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> <mo fence="true" stretchy="true"
   symmetric="true"></mo> </mrow> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)={\begin{cases}1\quad
   {\text{for}}\quad xy\neq 0\\0\quad {\text{for}}\quad
   xy=0\end{cases}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)={\begin{cases}1\quad {\text{for}}\quad xy\neq
   0\\0\quad {\text{for}}\quad xy=0\end{cases}}} where
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac
   linethickness="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to 0}
   \atop {y\to 0}}f(x,y)=1}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to 0} \atop {y\to 0}}f(x,y)=1} but
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo>,</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to
   (0,0)}f(x,y)}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to (0,0)}f(x,y)} does not exist.

   If the domain of f is restricted to
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>S</mi> <mo class="MJX-variant">∖</mo> <mo fence="false"
   stretchy="false">{</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mo fence="false"
   stretchy="false">}</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>×</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo class="MJX-variant">∖</mo> <mo
   fence="false" stretchy="false">{</mo> <mi>q</mi> <mo fence="false"
   stretchy="false">}</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>,</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (S\setminus \{p\})\times
   (T\setminus \{q\}),}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (S\setminus \{p\})\times (T\setminus \{q\}),} then the
   two definitions of limits coincide.^[354][15]

Multiple limits at infinity

   [[355]edit]

   The concept of multiple limit can extend to the limit at infinity, in a
   way similar to that of a single variable function. For
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>S</mi>
   <mo>×</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R}
   ,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R} ,} we say the double limit of
   f as x and y approaches infinity is L, written
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac
   linethickness="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </munder>
   <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to
   \infty } \atop {y\to \infty }}f(x,y)=L}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to \infty } \atop {y\to \infty }}f(x,y)=L}

   if the following condition holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a c > 0 such that for all x in S and y in
   T, whenever x > c and y > c, we have |f(x, y) − L| < ε.

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>c</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>></mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>∧</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>y</mi> <mo>></mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo>
   <mi>L</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
   </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>ε</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon
   >0)\,(\exists c>0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,((x>c)\land
   (y>c)\implies |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon ).}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists c>0)\,(\forall x\in
   S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,((x>c)\land (y>c)\implies |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon
   ).}

   We say the double limit of f as x and y approaches minus infinity is L,
   written
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac
   linethickness="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi>
   </mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi>
   </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to
   -\infty } \atop {y\to -\infty }}f(x,y)=L}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{{x\to -\infty } \atop {y\to -\infty }}f(x,y)=L}

   if the following condition holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a c > 0 such that x in S and y in T,
   whenever x < −c and y < −c, we have |f(x, y) − L| < ε.

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>c</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo><</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>∧</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>y</mi> <mo><</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo>
   <mi>L</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
   </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>ε</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon
   >0)\,(\exists c>0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,((x<-c)\land
   (y<-c)\implies |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon ).}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists c>0)\,(\forall x\in
   S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,((x<-c)\land (y<-c)\implies
   |f(x,y)-L|<\varepsilon ).}

Pointwise limits and uniform limits

   [[356]edit]

   Main articles: [357]Pointwise convergence and [358]Uniform convergence

   Let
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>S</mi>
   <mo>×</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R}
   .}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R} .} Instead of taking limit as
   (x, y) → (p, q), we may consider taking the limit of just one variable,
   say, x → p, to obtain a single-variable function of y, namely
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>g</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>T</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle g:T\to \mathbb
   {R} .}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle g:T\to \mathbb {R} .} In fact, this limiting process can
   be done in two distinct ways. The first one is called pointwise limit.
   We say the pointwise limit of f as x approaches p is g, denoted
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x,y)=g(y),}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x,y)=g(y),} or
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>pointwise</mtext> </mrow> <mo>.</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x,y)=g(y)\;\;{\text{pointwise}}.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x,y)=g(y)\;\;{\text{pointwise}}.}

   Alternatively, we may say f tends to g pointwise as x approaches p,
   denoted
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>as</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)\to
   g(y)\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to p,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)\to g(y)\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to p,} or
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>pointwise</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>as</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)\to
   g(y)\;\;{\text{pointwise}}\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to p.}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)\to
   g(y)\;\;{\text{pointwise}}\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to p.}

   This limit exists if the following holds:

   For every ε > 0 and every fixed y in T, there exists a δ(ε, y) > 0 such
   that for all x in S, whenever 0 < |x − p| < δ, we have |f(x, y) − g(y)|
   < ε.^[359][16]

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>δ</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn>
   <mo><</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
   </mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo>
   <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>ε</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon
   >0)\,(\forall y\in T)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in
   S)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta \implies |f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon ).}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\forall y\in T)\,(\exists
   \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta \implies
   |f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon ).}

   Here, δ = δ(ε, y) is a function of both ε and y. Each δ is chosen for a
   specific point of y. Hence we say the limit is pointwise in y. For
   example,
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mfrac> <mi>x</mi> <mrow> <mi>cos</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mi>y</mi> </mrow>
   </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)={\frac {x}{\cos
   y}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)={\frac {x}{\cos y}}} has a pointwise limit of
   constant zero function
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>pointwise</mtext> </mrow> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim
   _{x\to 0}f(x,y)=0(y)\;\;{\text{pointwise}}}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}f(x,y)=0(y)\;\;{\text{pointwise}}} because
   for every fixed y, the limit is clearly 0. This argument fails if y is
   not fixed: if y is very close to π/2, the value of the fraction may
   deviate from 0.

   This leads to another definition of limit, namely the uniform limit. We
   say the uniform limit of f on T as x approaches p is g, denoted
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <munder> <mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">u</mi> <mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">i</mi> <mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi> </mrow> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> </mrow> <mfrac linethickness="0"> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>p</mi> </mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo>∈</mo>
   <mi>T</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </munder> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle {\underset {{x\to p} \atop
   {y\in T}}{\mathrm {unif} \lim \;}}f(x,y)=g(y),}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle {\underset {{x\to p} \atop {y\in T}}{\mathrm {unif} \lim
   \;}}f(x,y)=g(y),} or
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>uniformly on</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>T</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x,y)=g(y)\;\;{\text{uniformly on}}\;T.}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x,y)=g(y)\;\;{\text{uniformly on}}\;T.}

   Alternatively, we may say f tends to g uniformly on T as x approaches
   p, denoted
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">⇉</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mtext>on</mtext> </mrow> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>T</mi>
   <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" />
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>as</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   f(x,y)\rightrightarrows g(y)\;{\text{on}}\;T\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to
   p,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)\rightrightarrows
   g(y)\;{\text{on}}\;T\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to p,} or
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>uniformly on</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>T</mi> <mspace width="thickmathspace" />
   <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mtext>as</mtext> </mrow> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>p</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)\to
   g(y)\;\;{\text{uniformly on}}\;T\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to
   p.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)\to g(y)\;\;{\text{uniformly
   on}}\;T\;\;{\text{as}}\;\;x\to p.}

   This limit exists if the following holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a δ(ε) > 0 such that for all x in S and y
   in T, whenever 0 < |x − p| < δ, we have |f(x, y) − g(y)| < ε.^[360][16]

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>δ</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn>
   <mo><</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
   </mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo>
   <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>ε</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon
   >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(\forall y\in
   T)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta \implies |f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon ).}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall
   x\in S)\,(\forall y\in T)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta \implies
   |f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon ).}

   Here, δ = δ(ε) is a function of only ε but not y. In other words, δ is
   uniformly applicable to all y in T. Hence we say the limit is uniform
   in y. For example,
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mi>cos</mi> <mo>⁡</mo>
   <mi>y</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f(x,y)=x\cos
   y}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)=x\cos y} has a uniform limit of constant zero
   function
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext> uniformly on</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   0}f(x,y)=0(y)\;\;{\text{ uniformly on}}\;\mathbb {R} }</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}f(x,y)=0(y)\;\;{\text{ uniformly
   on}}\;\mathbb {R} } because for all real y, cos y is bounded between
   [−1, 1]. Hence no matter how y behaves, we may use the [361]sandwich
   theorem to show that the limit is 0.

Iterated limits

   [[362]edit]

   Main article: [363]Iterated limits

   Let
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>S</mi>
   <mo>×</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R}
   .}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R} .} We may consider taking the
   limit of just one variable, say, x → p, to obtain a single-variable
   function of y, namely
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>g</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>T</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle g:T\to \mathbb
   {R} ,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle g:T\to \mathbb {R} ,} and then take limit in the other
   variable, namely y → q, to get a number L. Symbolically,
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>q</mi> </mrow> </munder> <munder> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo>
   <munder> <mo movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>q</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> <mo>.</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{y\to q}\lim _{x\to
   p}f(x,y)=\lim _{y\to q}g(y)=L.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{y\to q}\lim _{x\to p}f(x,y)=\lim _{y\to
   q}g(y)=L.}

   This limit is known as iterated limit of the multivariable
   function.^[364][17] The order of taking limits may affect the result,
   i.e.,

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>q</mi> </mrow> </munder> <munder> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>≠</mo>
   <munder> <mo movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>q</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{y\to q}\lim _{x\to
   p}f(x,y)\neq \lim _{x\to p}\lim _{y\to q}f(x,y)}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{y\to q}\lim _{x\to p}f(x,y)\neq \lim _{x\to
   p}\lim _{y\to q}f(x,y)} in general.

   A sufficient condition of equality is given by the [365]Moore-Osgood
   theorem, which requires the limit
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x,y)=g(y)}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x,y)=g(y)} to be uniform on T.^[366][18]

Functions on metric spaces

   [[367]edit]

   Suppose M and N are subsets of [368]metric spaces A and B,
   respectively, and f : M → N is defined between M and N, with x ∈ M, p a
   [369]limit point of M and L ∈ N. It is said that the limit of f as x
   approaches p is L and write

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x)=L}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x)=L}

   if the following property holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a δ > 0 such that for all points x ∈ M, 0
   < d[A](x, p) < δ implies d[B](f(x), L) < ε.^[370][19]

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>δ</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>M</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo> <msub> <mi>d</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>A</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <msub> <mi>d</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>B</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>,</mo> <mi>L</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo><</mo> <mi>ε</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon
   >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in M)\,(0<d_{A}(x,p)<\delta
   \implies d_{B}(f(x),L)<\varepsilon ).}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall
   x\in M)\,(0<d_{A}(x,p)<\delta \implies d_{B}(f(x),L)<\varepsilon ).}

   Again, note that p need not be in the domain of f, nor does L need to
   be in the range of f, and even if f(p) is defined it need not be equal
   to L.

Euclidean metric

   [[371]edit]

   The limit in [372]Euclidean space is a direct generalization of limits
   to [373]vector-valued functions. For example, we may consider a
   function
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>S</mi>
   <mo>×</mo> <mi>T</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <msup> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R} ^{3}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f:S\times T\to \mathbb {R} ^{3}} such that
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <msub>
   <mi>f</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   f(x,y)=(f_{1}(x,y),f_{2}(x,y),f_{3}(x,y)).}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x,y)=(f_{1}(x,y),f_{2}(x,y),f_{3}(x,y)).} Then, under
   the usual [374]Euclidean metric,
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>q</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <msub>
   <mi>L</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>L</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn>
   </mrow> </msub> <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>L</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to
   (p,q)}f(x,y)=(L_{1},L_{2},L_{3})}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to (p,q)}f(x,y)=(L_{1},L_{2},L_{3})} if the
   following holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a δ > 0 such that for all x in S and y in
   T,
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0"> <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <msqrt> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo

   </mrow> </msup> </msqrt> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\textstyle 0<{\sqrt
   {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}<\delta }</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\textstyle 0<{\sqrt {(x-p)^{2}+(y-q)^{2}}}<\delta } implies
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <msqrt> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo>−</mo> <msub>

   <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow>
   </msub> <mo>−</mo> <msub> <mi>L</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub> <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow

   <msup> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </msqrt> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>ε</mi>
   <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\textstyle {\sqrt
   {(f_{1}-L_{1})^{2}+(f_{2}-L_{2})^{2}+(f_{3}-L_{3})^{2}}}<\varepsilon
   .}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\textstyle {\sqrt
   {(f_{1}-L_{1})^{2}+(f_{2}-L_{2})^{2}+(f_{3}-L_{3})^{2}}}<\varepsilon .}
   ^[375][20]

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle

   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall

   {(f_{1}-L_{1})^{2}+(f_{2}-L_{2})^{2}+(f_{3}-L_{3})^{2}}}<\varepsilon

   In this example, the function concerned are finite-[376]dimension
   vector-valued function. In this case, the limit theorem for
   vector-valued function states that if the limit of each component
   exists, then the limit of a vector-valued function equals the vector
   with each component taken the limit:^[377][20]

   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-OPEN"> <mo

   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo

   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-CLOSE"> <mo maxsize="1.623em"
   minsize="1.623em">)</mo> </mrow> </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mrow> <mo>(</mo>
   <mrow> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo
