   <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow>
   </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>,</mo> <munder> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>p</mi>
   <mo>,</mo> <mi>q</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </munder>
   <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow>
   </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>,</mo> <munder> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>p</mi>
   <mo>,</mo> <mi>q</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </munder>
   <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>3</mn> </mrow>
   </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> <mo>)</mo> </mrow> <mo>.</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to
   (p,q)}{\Bigl (}f_{1}(x,y),f_{2}(x,y),f_{3}(x,y){\Bigr )}=\left(\lim
   _{(x,y)\to (p,q)}f_{1}(x,y),\lim _{(x,y)\to (p,q)}f_{2}(x,y),\lim
   _{(x,y)\to (p,q)}f_{3}(x,y)\right).}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{(x,y)\to (p,q)}{\Bigl
   (}f_{1}(x,y),f_{2}(x,y),f_{3}(x,y){\Bigr )}=\left(\lim _{(x,y)\to
   (p,q)}f_{1}(x,y),\lim _{(x,y)\to (p,q)}f_{2}(x,y),\lim _{(x,y)\to
   (p,q)}f_{3}(x,y)\right).}

Manhattan metric

   [[378]edit]

   One might also want to consider spaces other than Euclidean space. An
   example would be the Manhattan space. Consider
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo>:</mo> <mi>S</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <msup> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f:S\to \mathbb
   {R} ^{2}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f:S\to \mathbb {R} ^{2}} such that
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn>
   </mrow> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   f(x)=(f_{1}(x),f_{2}(x)).}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x)=(f_{1}(x),f_{2}(x)).} Then, under the
   [379]Manhattan metric,
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <msub> <mi>L</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo>,</mo> <msub>
   <mi>L</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x)=(L_{1},L_{2})}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x)=(L_{1},L_{2})} if the following
   holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a δ > 0 such that for all x in S, 0 < |x
   − p| < δ implies |f[1] − L[1]| + |f[2] − L[2]| < ε.

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>δ</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo>−</mo> <msub>
   <mi>L</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow>
   <mo>+</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo>
   </mrow> <msub> <mi>f</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn>
   </mrow> </msub> <mo>−</mo> <msub> <mi>L</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo>
   <mi>ε</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall
   \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta
   \implies |f_{1}-L_{1}|+|f_{2}-L_{2}|<\varepsilon ).}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall
   x\in S)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta \implies
   |f_{1}-L_{1}|+|f_{2}-L_{2}|<\varepsilon ).}

   Since this is also a finite-dimension vector-valued function, the limit
   theorem stated above also applies.^[380][21]

Uniform metric

   [[381]edit]

   Finally, we will discuss the limit in [382]function space, which has
   infinite dimensions. Consider a function f(x, y) in the function space
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>S</mi> <mo>×</mo> <mi>T</mi>
   <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle S\times T\to
   \mathbb {R} .}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle S\times T\to \mathbb {R} .} We want to find out as x
   approaches p, how f(x, y) will tend to another function g(y), which is
   in the function space
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>T</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle T\to \mathbb
   {R} .}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle T\to \mathbb {R} .} The "closeness" in this function
   space may be measured under the [383]uniform metric.^[384][22] Then, we
   will say the uniform limit of f on T as x approaches p is g and write
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <munder> <mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">u</mi> <mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">i</mi> <mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi> </mrow> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> </mrow> <mfrac linethickness="0"> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>p</mi> </mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo>∈</mo>
   <mi>T</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </munder> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle {\underset {{x\to p} \atop
   {y\in T}}{\mathrm {unif} \lim \;}}f(x,y)=g(y),}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle {\underset {{x\to p} \atop {y\in T}}{\mathrm {unif} \lim
   \;}}f(x,y)=g(y),} or
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>uniformly on</mtext> </mrow> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>T</mi> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x,y)=g(y)\;\;{\text{uniformly on}}\;T,}</annotation> </semantics>
   :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x,y)=g(y)\;\;{\text{uniformly on}}\;T,}

   if the following holds:

   For every ε > 0, there exists a δ > 0 such that for all x in S, 0 < |x
   − p| < δ implies
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">sup</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo>
   <mi>ε</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \sup _{y\in
   T}|f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon .}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \sup _{y\in T}|f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon .}

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>ε</mi> <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∃</mi> <mi>δ</mi>
   <mo>></mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace
   width="thinmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∀</mi> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>S</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thinmathspace" /> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mo><</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>−</mo> <mi>p</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo> <mi>δ</mi> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <mo stretchy="false">⟹</mo> <mspace
   width="thickmathspace" /> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">sup</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi>
   <mo>∈</mo> <mi>T</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>,</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mo><</mo>
   <mi>ε</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (\forall
   \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall x\in S)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta
   \implies \sup _{y\in T}|f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon ).}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle (\forall \varepsilon >0)\,(\exists \delta >0)\,(\forall
   x\in S)\,(0<|x-p|<\delta \implies \sup _{y\in
   T}|f(x,y)-g(y)|<\varepsilon ).}

   In fact, one can see that this definition is equivalent to that of the
   uniform limit of a multivariable function introduced in the previous
   section.

Functions on topological spaces

   [[385]edit]

   See also: [386]Filters in topology § Limits of functions

   Suppose X and Y are [387]topological spaces with Y a [388]Hausdorff
   space. Let p be a [389]limit point of Ω ⊆ X, and L ∈ Y. For a function
   f : Ω → Y, it is said that the limit of f as x approaches p is L,
   written

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>p</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   p}f(x)=L,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to p}f(x)=L,}

   if the following property holds:

   For every open [390]neighborhood V of L, there exists an open
   neighborhood U of p such that f(U ∩ Ω − {p}) ⊆ V.

   This last part of the definition can also be phrased "there exists an
   open [391]punctured neighbourhood U of p such that f(U ∩ Ω) ⊆ V".

   The domain of f does not need to contain p. If it does, then the value
   of f at p is irrelevant to the definition of the limit. In particular,
   if the domain of f is X − {p} (or all of X), then the limit of f as x →
   p exists and is equal to L if, for all subsets Ω of X with limit point
   p, the limit of the restriction of f to Ω exists and is equal to L.
   Sometimes this criterion is used to establish the non-existence of the
   two-sided limit of a function on ⁠
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \mathbb {R}
   }</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \mathbb {R} } ⁠ by showing that the [392]one-sided
   limits either fail to exist or do not agree. Such a view is fundamental
   in the field of [393]general topology, where limits and continuity at a
   point are defined in terms of special families of subsets, called
   [394]filters, or generalized sequences known as [395]nets.

   Alternatively, the requirement that Y be a Hausdorff space can be
   relaxed to the assumption that Y be a general topological space, but
   then the limit of a function may not be unique. In particular, one can
   no longer talk about the limit of a function at a point, but rather a
   limit or the set of limits at a point.

   A function is continuous at a limit point p of and in its domain if and
   only if f(p) is the (or, in the general case, a) limit of f(x) as x
   tends to p.

   There is another type of limit of a function, namely the sequential
   limit. Let f : X → Y be a mapping from a topological space X into a
   Hausdorff space Y, p ∈ X a limit point of X and L ∈ Y. The sequential
   limit of f as x tends to p is L if

          For every [396]sequence (x[n]) in X − {p} that [397]converges to
          p, the sequence f(x[n]) [398]converges to L.

   If L is the limit (in the sense above) of f as x approaches p, then it
   is a sequential limit as well, however the converse need not hold in
   general. If in addition X is [399]metrizable, then L is the sequential
   limit of f as x approaches p if and only if it is the limit (in the
   sense above) of f as x approaches p.

Other characterizations

   [[400]edit]

In terms of sequences

   [[401]edit]

   For functions on the real line, one way to define the limit of a
   function is in terms of the limit of sequences. (This definition is
   usually attributed to [402]Eduard Heine.) In this setting:
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   a}f(x)=L}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to a}f(x)=L} if, and only if, for all sequences
   x[n] (with x[n] not equal to a for all n) converging to a the sequence
   f(x[n]) converges to L. It was shown by [403]Sierpiński in 1916 that
   proving the equivalence of this definition and the definition above,
   requires and is equivalent to a weak form of the [404]axiom of choice.
   Note that defining what it means for a sequence x[n] to converge to a
   requires the [405]epsilon, delta method.

   Similarly as it was the case of Weierstrass's definition, a more
   general Heine definition applies to functions defined on [406]subsets
   of the real line. Let f be a real-valued function with the domain Dm(f
   ). Let a be the limit of a sequence of elements of Dm(f ) \ {a}. Then
   the limit (in this sense) of f is L as x approaches p if for every
   sequence x[n] ∈ Dm(f ) \ {a} (so that for all n, x[n] is not equal to
   a) that converges to a, the sequence f(x[n]) converges to L. This is
   the same as the definition of a sequential limit in the preceding
   section obtained by regarding the subset Dm(f ) of ⁠
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi
   mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \mathbb {R}
   }</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \mathbb {R} } ⁠ as a metric space with the induced
   metric.

In non-standard calculus

   [[407]edit]

   In non-standard calculus the limit of a function is defined by:
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   a}f(x)=L}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to a}f(x)=L} if and only if for all
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <msup> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo>∗</mo> </mrow> </msup> <mo>,</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle x\in \mathbb {R}
   ^{*},}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle x\in \mathbb {R} ^{*},}
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <msup> <mi>f</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo>∗</mo> </mrow> </msup> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>−</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f^{*}(x)-L}</annotation>
   </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f^{*}(x)-L} is infinitesimal whenever x − a is
   infinitesimal. Here
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <msup> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo>∗</mo> </mrow> </msup> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \mathbb
   {R} ^{*}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \mathbb {R} ^{*}} are the [408]hyperreal numbers and f*
   is the natural extension of f to the non-standard real numbers.
   [409]Keisler proved that such a hyperreal [410]definition of limit
   reduces the quantifier complexity by two quantifiers.^[411][23] On the
   other hand, Hrbacek writes that for the definitions to be valid for all
   hyperreal numbers they must implicitly be grounded in the ε-δ method,
   and claims that, from the pedagogical point of view, the hope that
   non-standard calculus could be done without ε-δ methods cannot be
   realized in full.^[412][24] Bŀaszczyk et al. detail the usefulness of
   [413]microcontinuity in developing a transparent definition of uniform
   continuity, and characterize Hrbacek's criticism as a "dubious
   lament".^[414][25]

In terms of nearness

   [[415]edit]

   At the 1908 international congress of mathematics [416]F. Riesz
   introduced an alternate way defining limits and continuity in concept
   called "nearness".^[417][26] A point x is defined to be near a set
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>A</mi> <mo>⊆</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle A\subseteq \mathbb {R}
   }</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle A\subseteq \mathbb {R} } if for every r > 0 there is a
   point a ∈ A so that |x − a| < r. In this setting the
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>L</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   a}f(x)=L}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to a}f(x)=L} if and only if for all
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>A</mi> <mo>⊆</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi> </mrow>
   <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle A\subseteq \mathbb {R}
   ,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle A\subseteq \mathbb {R} ,} L is near f(A) whenever a is
   near A. Here f(A) is the set
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mo fence="false"
   stretchy="false">{</mo> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mo stretchy="false">|</mo> </mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo> <mi>A</mi>
   <mo fence="false" stretchy="false">}</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \{f(x)|x\in
   A\}.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \{f(x)|x\in A\}.} This definition can also be extended
   to metric and topological spaces.

Relationship to continuity

   [[418]edit]

   Main article: [419]Continuous function

   The notion of the limit of a function is very closely related to the
   concept of continuity. A function f is said to be [420]continuous at c
   if it is both defined at c and its value at c equals the limit of f as
   x approaches c:

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   c}f(x)=f(c).}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to c}f(x)=f(c).} We have here assumed that c is
   a [421]limit point of the domain of f.

Properties

   [[422]edit]

   If a function f is real-valued, then the limit of f at p is L if and
   only if both the right-handed limit and left-handed limit of f at p
   exist and are equal to L.^[423][27]

   The function f is [424]continuous at p if and only if the limit of f(x)
   as x approaches p exists and is equal to f(p). If f : M → N is a
   function between metric spaces M and N, then it is equivalent that f
   transforms every sequence in M which converges towards p into a
   sequence in N which converges towards f(p).

   If N is a [425]normed vector space, then the limit operation is linear
   in the following sense: if the limit of f(x) as x approaches p is L and
   the limit of g(x) as x approaches p is P, then the limit of f(x) + g(x)
   as x approaches p is L + P. If a is a scalar from the base [426]field,
   then the limit of af(x) as x approaches p is aL.

   If f and g are real-valued (or complex-valued) functions, then taking
   the limit of an operation on f(x) and g(x) (e.g., f + g, f − g, f × g,
   f / g, f^ g) under certain conditions is compatible with the operation
   of limits of f(x) and g(x). This fact is often called the algebraic
   limit theorem. The main condition needed to apply the following rules
   is that the limits on the right-hand sides of the equations exist (in
   other words, these limits are finite values including 0). Additionally,
   the identity for division requires that the denominator on the
   right-hand side is non-zero (division by 0 is not defined), and the
   identity for exponentiation requires that the base is positive, or zero
   while the exponent is positive (finite).

   or −∞. In each rule above, when one of the limits on the right is ∞ or
   −∞, the limit on the left may sometimes still be determined by the
   following rules.

   }}q>0\end{cases}}\\[4pt]q^{\infty }&=&{\begin{cases}0&{\text{if
   }}0<q<1\\\infty &{\text{if }}q>1\end{cases}}\\[4pt]q^{-\infty

   }}q>1\end{cases}}\end{array}}}

   (see also [427]Extended real number line).

   In other cases the limit on the left may still exist, although the
   right-hand side, called an [428]indeterminate form, does not allow one
   to determine the result. This depends on the functions f and g. These
   indeterminate forms are:

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mtable columnalign="center center" rowspacing="1em 1.2em 1.2em 0.4em"
   columnspacing="1em"> <mtr> <mtd> <mstyle displaystyle="true"
   scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mn>0</mn>
   <mn>0</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mtd> <mtd> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>±</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow>
   <mrow> <mo>±</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow> </mfrac>
   </mrow> </mstyle> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> <mo>×</mo>
   <mo>±</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mtd> <mtd> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mspace
   width="2em" /> <msup> <mn>0</mn> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mspace width="2em" /> </mtd> <mtd> <mspace
   width="2em" /> <msup> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </msup> <mspace width="2em"
   /> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <msup> <mn>1</mn> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo>±</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi>
   </mrow> </msup> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   {\begin{array}{cc}\displaystyle {\frac {0}{0}}&\displaystyle {\frac
   {\pm \infty }{\pm \infty }}\\[6pt]0\times \pm \infty &\infty +-\infty
   \\[8pt]\qquad 0^{0}\qquad &\qquad \infty ^{0}\qquad \\[8pt]1^{\pm
   \infty }\end{array}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle {\begin{array}{cc}\displaystyle {\frac
   {0}{0}}&\displaystyle {\frac {\pm \infty }{\pm \infty }}\\[6pt]0\times
   \pm \infty &\infty +-\infty \\[8pt]\qquad 0^{0}\qquad &\qquad \infty
   ^{0}\qquad \\[8pt]1^{\pm \infty }\end{array}}}

   See further [429]L'Hôpital's rule below and [430]Indeterminate form.

Limits of compositions of functions

   [[431]edit]

   In general, from knowing that
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>y</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>b</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>c</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{y\to
   b}f(y)=c}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{y\to b}f(y)=c} and
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>g</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>b</mi> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   a}g(x)=b,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to a}g(x)=b,} it does not follow that
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   a}f(g(x))=c.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to a}f(g(x))=c.} However, this "chain rule"
   does hold if one of the following additional conditions holds:
     * f(b) = c (that is, f is continuous at b), or
     * g does not take the value b near a (that is, there exists a δ > 0
       such that if 0 < |x − a| < δ then |g(x) − b| > 0).

   As an example of this phenomenon, consider the following function that
   violates both additional restrictions:

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mrow> <mo>{</mo> <mtable columnalign="left left" rowspacing=".2em"
   columnspacing="1em" displaystyle="false"> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> </mtd>
   <mtd> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>if </mtext> </mrow>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>≠</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>1</mn>
   </mtd> <mtd> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>if </mtext> </mrow>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> <mo
   fence="true" stretchy="true" symmetric="true"></mo> </mrow> </mrow>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   f(x)=g(x)={\begin{cases}0&{\text{if }}x\neq 0\\1&{\text{if
   }}x=0\end{cases}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(x)=g(x)={\begin{cases}0&{\text{if }}x\neq
   0\\1&{\text{if }}x=0\end{cases}}}

   Since the value at f(0) is a [432]removable discontinuity,
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   a}f(x)=0}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to a}f(x)=0} for all a. Thus, the naïve chain
   rule would suggest that the limit of f(f(x)) is 0. However, it is the
   case that
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mrow> <mo>{</mo> <mtable
   columnalign="left left" rowspacing=".2em" columnspacing="1em"
   displaystyle="false"> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> <mtd> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>if </mtext> </mrow> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>≠</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> <mtd>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mtext>if </mtext> </mrow> <mi>x</mi>
   <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> <mo fence="true"
   stretchy="true" symmetric="true"></mo> </mrow> </mrow> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   f(f(x))={\begin{cases}1&{\text{if }}x\neq 0\\0&{\text{if
   }}x=0\end{cases}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle f(f(x))={\begin{cases}1&{\text{if }}x\neq 0\\0&{\text{if
   }}x=0\end{cases}}} and so
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
   <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   a}f(f(x))=1}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to a}f(f(x))=1} for all a.

Rational functions

   [[435]edit]

   For n a nonnegative integer and constants
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <msub> <mi>a</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo>,</mo> <msub>
   <mi>a</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>a</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>3</mn>
   </mrow> </msub> <mo>,</mo> <mo>…</mo> <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>a</mi>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> </mrow> </msub> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},\ldots ,a_{n}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},\ldots ,a_{n}} and
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <msub> <mi>b</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub> <mo>,</mo> <msub>
   <mi>b</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>b</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>3</mn>
   </mrow> </msub> <mo>,</mo> <mo>…</mo> <mo>,</mo> <msub> <mi>b</mi>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo>,</mo>
   </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},\ldots
   ,b_{n},}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},\ldots ,b_{n},}

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow>
   </munder> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow> <msub>
   <mi>a</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> </mrow>
   </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>a</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow>
   </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>a</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msub> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow>
   </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo>⋯</mo> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>a</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> </mrow> </msub> </mrow> <mrow>
   <msub> <mi>b</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow>
   </msub> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi>
   </mrow> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>b</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msub> <msup> <mi>x</mi>
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow>
   </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>b</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </msub> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> <mo>−</mo> <mn>2</mn> </mrow>
   </msup> <mo>+</mo> <mo>⋯</mo> <mo>+</mo> <msub> <mi>b</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> </mrow> </msub> </mrow> </mfrac>
   </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <msub>
   <mi>a</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msub>
   <msub> <mi>b</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mn>1</mn> </mrow>
   </msub> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to \infty }{\frac
   {a_{1}x^{n}+a_{2}x^{n-1}+a_{3}x^{n-2}+\dots
   +a_{n}}{b_{1}x^{n}+b_{2}x^{n-1}+b_{3}x^{n-2}+\dots +b_{n}}}={\frac
   {a_{1}}{b_{1}}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to \infty }{\frac
   {a_{1}x^{n}+a_{2}x^{n-1}+a_{3}x^{n-2}+\dots
   +a_{n}}{b_{1}x^{n}+b_{2}x^{n-1}+b_{3}x^{n-2}+\dots +b_{n}}}={\frac
   {a_{1}}{b_{1}}}}

   This can be proven by dividing both the numerator and denominator by
   x^n. If the numerator is a polynomial of higher degree, the limit does
   not exist. If the denominator is of higher degree, the limit is 0.

Exponential functions

   [[437]edit]

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mtable columnalign="left center left" rowspacing="0.8em 0.8em 0.8em
   0.8em 0.4em" columnspacing="1em"> <mtr> <mtd> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mi>x</mi> <msup> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac>
   <mn>1</mn> <mi>x</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </msup> </mstyle> </mtd> <mtd>
   <mo>=</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
   <munder> <mo movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>r</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow> </munder> <msup> <mrow> <mo>(</mo>
   <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac>
   <mn>1</mn> <mi>r</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </mrow> <mo>)</mo> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>r</mi> </mrow> </msup> <mo>=</mo>
   <mi>e</mi> </mstyle> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"

   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>e</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </msup> <mo>−</mo>
   <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mtd> <mtd>
   <mo>=</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr> <mtd> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mi>e</mi> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>a</mi> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </msup>
   <mo>−</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>b</mi> <mi>x</mi> </mrow>
   </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>=</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mfrac> <mi>a</mi> <mi>b</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mtd> </mtr>
   <mtr> <mtd> <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac>
   <mrow> <msup> <mi>c</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>a</mi>
   <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </msup> <mo>−</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow>
   <mi>b</mi> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mtd> <mtd>
   <mo>=</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
   <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mi>a</mi> <mi>b</mi> </mfrac>
   </mrow> <mi>ln</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mi>c</mi> </mstyle> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr>
   <mtd> <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo
   movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo> <msup>
   <mn>0</mn> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo>+</mo> </mrow> </msup>
   </mrow> </munder> <msup> <mi>x</mi> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </msup> </mstyle> </mtd> <mtd> <mo>=</mo> </mtd>
   <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> </mtr> </mtable> </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow>
   <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
   {\begin{array}{lcl}\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}(1+x)^{\frac
   {1}{x}}&=&\displaystyle \lim _{r\to \infty }\left(1+{\frac
   {1}{r}}\right)^{r}=e\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac
   {e^{x}-1}{x}}&=&1\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac
   {e^{ax}-1}{bx}}&=&\displaystyle {\frac {a}{b}}\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim
   _{x\to 0}{\frac {c^{ax}-1}{bx}}&=&\displaystyle {\frac {a}{b}}\ln
   c\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   0^{+}}x^{x}&=&1\end{array}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle {\begin{array}{lcl}\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   0}(1+x)^{\frac {1}{x}}&=&\displaystyle \lim _{r\to \infty
   }\left(1+{\frac {1}{r}}\right)^{r}=e\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
   0}{\frac {e^{x}-1}{x}}&=&1\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac
   {e^{ax}-1}{bx}}&=&\displaystyle {\frac {a}{b}}\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim
   _{x\to 0}{\frac {c^{ax}-1}{bx}}&=&\displaystyle {\frac {a}{b}}\ln
   c\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0^{+}}x^{x}&=&1\end{array}}}

Logarithmic functions

   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mtable columnalign="left center left" rowspacing="0.8em 0.8em 0.4em"
   columnspacing="1em"> <mtr> <mtd> <mstyle displaystyle="true"
   scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>ln</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> <mi>x</mi> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle>
   </mtd> <mtd> <mo>=</mo> </mtd> <mtd> <mn>1</mn> </mtd> </mtr> <mtr>
   <mtd> <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo

   <mrow> <mi>ln</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>1</mn>

   <mrow> <mi>b</mi> <mi>x</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mstyle> </mtd>

   \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac {\log _{c}(1+ax)}{bx}}&=&\displaystyle {\frac
   {a}{b\ln c}}\end{array}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle {\begin{array}{lcl}\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac
   {\ln(1+x)}{x}}&=&1\\[4pt]\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac
   {\ln(1+ax)}{bx}}&=&\displaystyle {\frac {a}{b}}\\[4pt]\displaystyle
   \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac {\log _{c}(1+ax)}{bx}}&=&\displaystyle {\frac
   {a}{b\ln c}}\end{array}}}

L'Hôpital's rule

   [[439]edit]

   Main article: [440]l'Hôpital's rule

   This rule uses [441]derivatives to find limits of [442]indeterminate
   forms 0/0 or ±∞/∞, and only applies to such cases. Other indeterminate
   forms may be manipulated into this form. Given two functions f(x) and
   g(x), defined over an [443]open interval I containing the desired limit
   point c, then if:
    1.
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo
       movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
       class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
       <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
       <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <munder> <mo
       movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
       class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
       <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
       <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn>
       <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to c}f(x)=\lim
       _{x\to c}g(x)=0,}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to c}f(x)=\lim _{x\to c}g(x)=0,} or
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo
       movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
       class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
       <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
       <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mo>±</mo>
       <munder> <mo movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
       class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
       <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
       <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>=</mo> <mo>±</mo> <mi
       mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow>
       <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to
       c}f(x)=\pm \lim _{x\to c}g(x)=\pm \infty ,}</annotation>
       </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to c}f(x)=\pm \lim _{x\to c}g(x)=\pm \infty
       ,} and
    2.
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>f</mi> </mstyle> </mrow>
       <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
       f}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle f} and
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>g</mi> </mstyle> </mrow>
       <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle
       g}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle g} are differentiable over
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>I</mi> <mo
       class="MJX-variant">∖</mo> <mo fence="false"
       stretchy="false">{</mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo fence="false"
       stretchy="false">}</mo> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle I\setminus
       \{c\},}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle I\setminus \{c\},} and
    3.
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <msup> <mi>g</mi> <mo>′</mo>
       </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo
       stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>≠</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mstyle> </mrow>
       <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle g'(x)\neq
       0}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle g'(x)\neq 0} for all
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <mi>x</mi> <mo>∈</mo>
       <mi>I</mi> <mo class="MJX-variant">∖</mo> <mo fence="false"
       stretchy="false">{</mo> <mi>c</mi> <mo fence="false"
       stretchy="false">}</mo> <mo>,</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle x\in I\setminus
       \{c\},}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle x\in I\setminus \{c\},} and
    4.
       [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo
       movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
       class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
       <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
       displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0"> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup>
       <mi>f</mi> <mo>′</mo> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
       <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <msup>
       <mi>g</mi> <mo>′</mo> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
       <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mstyle>
       </mrow> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
       encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to c}{\tfrac
       {f'(x)}{g'(x)}}}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
       {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to c}{\tfrac {f'(x)}{g'(x)}}} exists,

   then:
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>f</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow>
   <mrow> <mi>g</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <munder>
   <mo movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mi>c</mi> </mrow> </munder> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac>
   <mrow> <msup> <mi>f</mi> <mo>′</mo> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mi>g</mi>
   <mo>′</mo> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle>
   </mrow> <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim
   _{x\to c}{\frac {f(x)}{g(x)}}=\lim _{x\to c}{\frac
   {f'(x)}{g'(x)}}.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to c}{\frac {f(x)}{g(x)}}=\lim _{x\to c}{\frac
   {f'(x)}{g'(x)}}.}

   Normally, the first condition is the most important one.

   For example:
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>sin</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>sin</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <munder>
   <mo movablelimits="true" form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">→</mo>
   <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </munder> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac>
   <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>cos</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mn>2</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> <mrow>
   <mn>3</mn> <mi>cos</mi> <mo>⁡</mo> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mn>3</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </mfrac>
   </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mrow>
   <mn>2</mn> <mo>⋅</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>⋅</mo>
   <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mfrac> <mn>2</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mfrac> </mrow>
   <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac
   {\sin(2x)}{\sin(3x)}}=\lim _{x\to 0}{\frac
   {2\cos(2x)}{3\cos(3x)}}={\frac {2\cdot 1}{3\cdot 1}}={\frac
   {2}{3}}.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to 0}{\frac {\sin(2x)}{\sin(3x)}}=\lim _{x\to
   0}{\frac {2\cos(2x)}{3\cos(3x)}}={\frac {2\cdot 1}{3\cdot 1}}={\frac
   {2}{3}}.}

Summations and integrals

   [[444]edit]

   Specifying an infinite bound on a summation or integral is a common
   shorthand for specifying a limit.

   A short way to write the limit
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>n</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow>
   </munder> <munderover> <mo>∑</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mi>i</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mi>s</mi> </mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mi>n</mi> </mrow> </munderover> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
   <mi>i</mi> <mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{n\to \infty }\sum
   _{i=s}^{n}f(i)}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{n\to \infty }\sum _{i=s}^{n}f(i)} is
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munderover> <mo>∑</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>i</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mi>s</mi> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow>
   </munderover> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>i</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \sum _{i=s}^{\infty
   }f(i).}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \sum _{i=s}^{\infty }f(i).} An important example of
   limits of sums such as these are [445]series.

   A short way to write the limit
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow>
   </munder> <msubsup> <mo>∫</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mi>a</mi> </mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> </mrow>
   </msubsup> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>d</mi>
   <mi>t</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to \infty }\int
   _{a}^{x}f(t)\;dt}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to \infty }\int _{a}^{x}f(t)\;dt} is
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <msubsup> <mo>∫</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>a</mi> </mrow> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi> </mrow>
   </msubsup> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>d</mi>
   <mi>t</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \int _{a}^{\infty
   }f(t)\;dt.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \int _{a}^{\infty }f(t)\;dt.}

   A short way to write the limit
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <munder> <mo movablelimits="true"
   form="prefix">lim</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>x</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">→</mo> <mo>−</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi>
   </mrow> </munder> <msubsup> <mo>∫</mo> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
   <mi>x</mi> </mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>b</mi> </mrow>
   </msubsup> <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>d</mi>
   <mi>t</mi> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \lim _{x\to -\infty }\int
   _{x}^{b}f(t)\;dt}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \lim _{x\to -\infty }\int _{x}^{b}f(t)\;dt} is
   [MATH: <semantics> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mstyle
   displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0"> <msubsup> <mo>∫</mo> <mrow
   class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mo>−</mo> <mi mathvariant="normal">∞</mi>
   </mrow> <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD"> <mi>b</mi> </mrow> </msubsup>
   <mi>f</mi> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo> <mi>t</mi> <mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo> <mspace width="thickmathspace" /> <mi>d</mi>
   <mi>t</mi> <mo>.</mo> </mstyle> </mrow> <annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \int _{-\infty
   }^{b}f(t)\;dt.}</annotation> </semantics> :MATH]
   {\displaystyle \int _{-\infty }^{b}f(t)\;dt.}
